snap-18-late-desperate · repeat 2 · ModelRetry · 121 words · 9.3 s

Elias found the letter among his mother's things, creased soft from rereading. Ada's handwriting. The passes marked in Constance's careful pencil.

He read it three times at the workbench. Then he swept aside his half-finished parts and laid the letter flat, smoothing each fold with his thumb until it stayed down.

The caravan had gone out with too little. He started a list: parkas, spare fuel, more food, a lower route that avoided the high pass. He wrote a departure date, crossed it out, wrote another, crossed that out too.

By the time the lamp guttered, the list filled two pages.

Her sister was still waiting in the Wailing Moth village. Elias meant to bring Ada home to Wren's Hollow himself.

Judge: overall 4, voice 4, clarity 5, tone 5. A restrained scene that shows grief and Elias's neurotic, industrious traits through the list and the crossed-out dates, ending on a clear resolve. Its one weakness is that it states as fact that the caravan was underprovisioned, which the briefing does not support.
Invented: [minor] The caravan had gone out with too little. (The briefing blames the blizzard. Insufficient supplies is an implied cause stated as fact.) | [minor] The passes marked in Constance's careful pencil. (Embellishes the letter with markings that the briefing does not mention.) | [minor] a lower route that avoided the high pass (Adds route geography beyond the briefing's simple mention of 'the passes'.)

snap-18-late-desperate · repeat 3 · Model · 107 words · 12.2 s

Elias sat at his workbench in Wren's Hollow with Ada's letter flattened under a wrench, because his hands would not keep it still. He had read it four times. The creases were his mother's.

He checked the pass routes again, then the weather logs from Day 790, tallying the hours of snow in the margin. Twice he started a list of supplies, and twice he tore it off and began cleaner.

Somewhere past those passes, in a Wailing Moth village, Ada was still alive, still expecting her sister.

Elias pinned the list above the bench. He would finish the road his mother started and bring Ada home.

Judge: overall 4, voice 5, clarity 5, tone 5. A restrained, well-observed scene: the wrench pinning the letter, the creases, and the torn-up lists show Elias's grief and his Neurotic and Industrious traits without naming emotions, and it ends cleanly on his intent. The only drawback is the small unsupported assumption that Ada was expecting her sister.
Invented: [minor] still expecting her sister (The briefing never says Ada knew Constance was coming; this is an inference stated as fact.) | [minor] the weather logs from Day 790 (Implies the blizzard struck on Day 790, the day the caravan was recorded lost. Plausible, but not stated.)
